commissions paid to agents, advisers, brokers and dealers. Commissions may be paid to brokerage firms which provide

and databases or software in support of these services. These services form an integral part of the investment decision 
making process. The ascertainable soft dollar value of services received under the soft dollar arrangement entered into 
by the Manager for the six months period ended June 30, 2020 was $473 (June 30, 2019 - $155).

15.  REDEEMABLE UNITS 
The Partnership is authorized to issue 2,600,000 Founder Class units, 737,100 Class B units, 1,383,933 Class M units 
and an unlimited number of Class A, Class AVN, Class C, Class D, Class F, Class FNV, Class UF, Class UFNV, Class 
I, Class INV, Class J, Class JNV, Class UJ, and Class UJNV units at the net asset value per unit of the applicable class 
(collectively the “units”), (as defined in the LPA) or such amount as the General Partner may determine from time to 
time. These units carry equal voting rights, that entitle the unitholder to participate pro-rata in distributions and the 
Partnership’s class net assets attributable to unitholders, except for Class ANV, Class FNV, Class INV, Class JNV, 
Class UFNV and Class UJNV. The various classes of units are subject to different management fees and performance 
fees. The units are redeemable at the option of the unitholders, subject to certain restrictions as defined below. Effective 
May 27, 2019, the Partnership amended and restated the LPA to create new units denominated in U.S. dollars, Class 
UF, Class UJ, Class UFNV and Class UJNV. 
During the six months ended June 30, 2020, the Partnership issued units for consideration of $301,131 including switch-
ins and switch-outs (June 30, 2019 - $299,572) from the issuance of Class A units, Class ANV units, Class B units, 
Class F units, Class FNV units, Class UF units, Class UFNV units, Class J units, Class JNV units, Class UJ units and 
Class UJNV units.

As at June 30, 2020, the General Partner holds 802 (December 31, 2019 – 802) units in the Partnership. 
(a) Redemptions 
The limited partners have the right to redeem units on the last business day in March, June, September and 
December of each year at a redemption price per unit equal to the class net asset value per unit. The units must 
be surrendered for the quarterly redemption together with a redemption notice submitted by the limited partner to 
the General Partner at least 30 days prior to the applicable quarterly redemption date. Payment of redemption 
proceeds will be made within 60 days following the quarterly redemption date. The Partnership shall not accept 
redemptions on any quarterly redemption date, representing more than 5% of the average number of units of each 
class outstanding for the 90-day period immediately preceding that applicable quarterly redemption date. In the 
event that the number of units of each class tendered for redemption in respect to a quarterly redemption date 
exceeds the limits set forth above, the Partnership shall redeem such units tendered for redemption on a pro-rata 
basis. The General Partner may, in its sole discretion, waive the above limitations in respect of all units tendered 
for redemption in respect of any one or more quarterly redemption dates.  
As at June 30, 2020, redemptions payable was $59,529 (December 31, 2019 - $12,204). As at December 31, 
2019, Timbercreek Asset Management Inc. (“TAMI”), fully redeemed from Class FND units.

16.  RELATED PARTY TRANSACTIONS AND BALANCES 
Except as disclosed elsewhere in these interim consolidated financial statements, related party transactions and 
transactions with the Manger include the following transactions and balances. 
(a) As per the amended and restated fund management and investment advisory agreement with the Manager, the 
Partnership shall pay the following fees: 
(i) Management fee 
The Partnership pays a monthly management fee equal to 1/12 of the annual management fee percentage 
applicable to each class of units as defined below, plus applicable taxes, paid monthly in arrears based on the 
Net Asset Value (“Class NAV”) of such units at the last valuation date in each month. 
The management fee payable is 1.00% of Class NAV per annum for the Founder Class, Class B and Class M 
units; 1.25% of Class NAV per annum for Class I, Class INV, Class J , Class UJ, Class UJNV and Class JNV 
units; and 1.50% of Class NAV per annum for Class A, Class ANV, Class C, Class D, Class F, Class UF, Class 
UFNV and Class FNV units. For the six months ended June 30, 2020, the Partnership incurred management 
fees of $8,477 (June 30, 2019 – $4,092). As at June 30, 2020, $1,482 (December 31, 2019 – $1,313) was 
payable to the Manager.

(ii) Performance fee 
The Manager is entitled to a performance fee in respect of a unit if the total return of that class of units on a 
per unit basis for the 12 months ended on the performance valuation date of that year exceeds the amount 
resulting from multiplying the hurdle rate (the “Hurdle Rate”) applicable to the class, as specified below, to the 
high water mark (the “High Water Mark”) of that unit. If the unit has not been issued for a full year at the time 
of calculation, such calculation shall be pro-rated proportional to the number of days for which that unit has 
been outstanding. 
The amount of performance fees payable on a unit for a determination year, if the test mentioned above is 
satisfied, is equal to 20% of the amount by which: 
(1) the total return of the class of units to which that unit belongs, determined on a per-unit basis, during the 
year on the performance valuation date exceeds; and 
(2) the amount that results from multiplying the Hurdle Rate of the class of units to which that unit belongs 
by the High Water Mark of that unit. 
The High Water Mark for a unit is the higher of the following: 
(1) the initial issue price of that unit;  
(2) the Class NAV per unit (after deducting the performance fee payable by that class of units for the year 
prior to the determination year and any current or deferred tax liabilities) on the performance valuation 
date of the year before the determination year; and 
(3) the Class NAV per unit on the performance valuation date of the last annual period in respect of which a 
performance fee was paid in respect of such unit. 
The Hurdle Rate is 10% for Founder Class units and 8% for all other classes of units. 
The performance fee is payable to the Manager within 30 days of December 31 of each calendar year. As at 
June 30, 2020, $345 (December 31, 2019 – $5,354) in performance fee was payable relating to the Manager, 
including applicable taxes. For the period ended June 30, 2020, performance fee expense to the Partnership 
was $340 (June 30, 2019 - $4,108) and is different from the performance fee payable amount due to applicable 
input tax credit.  
(iii) Trailer fees 
The Partnership will pay each registered dealer of the Class A units and Class D units, a trailer fee equal to 
0.50% and 1.00%, respectively, of the Class NAV per unit per annum, calculated and paid at the end of each 
calendar quarter. 
During the period ended June 30, 2020, the Partnership incurred trailer fees of $56 (June 30, 2019 – $34). As 
at June 30, 2020, $28 of trailer fees (December 31, 2019 – $26) is included in accounts payable and other 
liabilities.

17. CAPITAL RISK MANAGEMENT 
 
The Manager manages the capital of the Partnership, which consists of the net assets of the Partnership, in accordance 
with the investment objectives set out in the LPA. The Partnership is not subject to externally imposed capital 
requirements. 
 
18. RISK MANAGEMENT 
In the normal course of business, the Partnership is exposed to a variety of financial risks, including credit risk, liquidity 
risk and market risk (including interest rate risk, market price risk and currency risk). The value of investments within 
the Partnership’s portfolio can fluctuate as a result of changes in general economic conditions and company news 
related to specific securities within the Partnership. The level of risk depends on the Partnership’s investment objectives 
and the nature of its investments. The Manager seeks to minimize the potential adverse effects of risk on the 
Partnership’s performance by retaining professional, experienced portfolio advisors and analysts who monitor the 
Partnership’s positions and market events as well as diversifying investments within the parameters of the investment 
objectives. To assist in managing risk, the Manager uses internal guidelines that identify the target exposures for each 
type of investment asset class while adhering to the investment restrictions of the Partnership. 
 (a) Credit risk 
Credit risk is the risk that the counterparty to a financial instrument will fail to discharge an obligation or commitment 
to the Partnership. The maximum exposure to credit risk is represented by total assets of the Partnership. 
For publicly-traded securities, the Partnership minimizes concentration risk by trading with a large number of credit-
worthy brokers and counterparties recognized on the various stock exchanges. The risk of default is considered 
minimal as all transactions are settled and paid for upon delivery using approved brokers. 
44
TIMBERCREEK FOUR QUADRANT GLOBAL REAL ESTATE PARTNERS 
Notes to the Interim Consolidated Financial Statements 
For the six months ended June 30, 2020 and 2019 
(in thousands of Canadian dollars, except unit, per unit amounts and where otherwise noted)  
(Unaudited)  
 
 
 
 
Credit risk may arise on private direct mortgage and loan investments, including interest receivable of $3,022 
(December 31, 2019 – $2,090), where the borrower may be unable to honour its loan commitments that could 
result in a loss to the Partnership. The Partnership mitigates this risk by: (i) adhering to the investment restrictions 
and investment objectives of the Partnership; (ii) ensuring a comprehensive due diligence process is conducted on 
each loan prior to funding, which generally includes, but is not limited to, engaging professional independent 
consultants, lawyers and appraisers, and performing credit checks on prospective borrowers; and (iii) actively 
monitoring the investment and initiating recovery procedures where required. 
Exposure to credit risk relating to forward contracts and equity swaps is concentrated to two counterparties, both 
of which have a S&P Global credit rating of ‘A’ or higher. The Partnership also provides collateral to these 
counterparties. The notional and fair value of the forward contracts and equity swaps are disclosed in note 10. 
Exposure to credit risk relating to the cash balance is concentrated to two custodian banks, both of which have a 
S&P Global credit rating of ‘A’ or higher.   
The Partnership is exposed to credit risk on total preferred share securities of $35,497 (December 31, 2019 – 
$37,070) which is disclosed in note 8. The preferred share securities held by the Partnership are unrated. Given 
the nature of the real estate industry, many issuers traditionally obtain debt financing through mortgages secured 
by real property and, in certain circumstances, will issue publicly-listed debentures. Issuers which do not have 
public debt securities are typically not rated by the rating agencies. As such, to minimize the risk associated with a 
fixed return, preferred share investment, the Manager conducts a thorough analysis of the issuer to determine their 
creditworthiness. Specifically, the Manager will conduct an analysis of each issuer analyzing the loan-to-value 
ratios, debt service coverage ratio and the quality of the issuer’s real estate holdings. 
(b) Liquidity risk 
Liquidity risk is defined as the risk that the Partnership may not be able to settle or meet its obligations associated 
with its financial liabilities. The Partnership’s exposure to liquidity risk is concentrated in the periodic cash 
redemptions of units. The Partnership has illiquid private investments, including direct real estate investments and 
debt investments, and it is possible that it may not be able to sell such positions without facing adverse pricing. To 
minimize this risk, the Manager has a targeted allocation of 20% to 50% of the Partnership’s total assets in private 
direct real estate and/or illiquid assets. Further, the Partnership has access to $55,467 (December 31, 2019 - 
$50,000) credit facility to enhance liquidity. In addition, the Partnership’s portfolio investments include listed 
securities which are considered to be readily realizable as they are actively traded on major stock exchanges, 
should short-term liquidity be required.

 (d) Price risk 
The Partnership is exposed to equity price risk for its investments in public securities. This arises from investments held 
by the Partnership for which prices in the future are uncertain. The Partnership’s policy is to manage price risk through 
diversification and the selection of securities within specified limits as set by our investment objectives. The Partnership 
intends to make investments in public securities, which include public equities and public debts, in the range of 20% to 
60% (range of 10% to 30% for each public equities and public debts) of total net assets attributable to unitholders. The 
Partnership’s policy requires that the overall market position is monitored on a daily basis by the Manager. The 
Partnership had no concentration in individual public securities positions exceeding 5% of the net assets attributable to 
unitholders. Based on the Partnership’s investments in public securities as at June 30, 2020, if the prices of public 
securities had been 5% higher or lower, with all other variables held constant, the net assets attributable to unitholders 
would have been increased or decreased by $20,270 (December 31, 2019 – $16,630). 
(e) Interest rate risk 
Interest rate risk arises from the possibility that changes in interest rates will affect future cash flows or fair values of 
financial instruments. 
Interest rate risk arises when the Partnership invests in interest-bearing financial instruments. The Partnership is 
exposed to the risk that the value of such financial instruments will fluctuate due to changes in the prevailing levels of 
market interest rates. The Partnership’s private debt investments held through the Partnership bear interest at fixed 
rates, excluding 33 mortgage investments amounting to $145,015 (December 31, 2019 - 30 mortgages, $152,232) that 
bears interest at a variable rate, with certain mortgages having a “floor rate”. If there was a decrease or increase of 
0.50% in interest rates, with all other variable constant, the impact from variable rate debt investment would be a 
decrease or increase in net income of $45 or $363, respectively (June 30, 2019 - decrease or increase of $95 or $249). 
The Partnership also held mortgages and credit facilities with variable rate through its investments, amounting to 
$129,194 (December 31, 2019 - $84,135), excluding the capitalized financing costs. If there was a decrease or increase 
of 0.50% in interest rates, with all other variable constant, the impact from variable rate mortgages would be a decrease 
or increase in net income of $188 or $323, respectively (June 30, 2019 – decrease or increase of $48 or $48).  There 
is minimal sensitivity to interest rate fluctuations on any cash and cash equivalents invested at short-term market interest 
rates.

Investments classified within Level 3 are valued at each reporting date by the Manager’s valuation committee 
supplemented with independent appraisals where available. The Manager considers the appropriateness of the 
valuation model inputs, as well as the valuation results using various valuation methods and techniques generally 
recognized as standard within the industry. 
The valuation techniques and the inputs used for the Partnership’s financial instruments are as follows: 
(a) Mortgage and loan investments 
There is no quoted price in an active market for the mortgage and loan investments. The Manager makes its 
determination of fair value based on its assessment of the current lending market for mortgage and loan investments 
of the same or similar terms. Typically, the fair value of these mortgage and loan investments approximate their carrying 
values, given the amounts consist of short-term loans that are repayable at the option of the borrower without yield 
maintenance or penalties. As a result, the fair value of mortgage and loan investments is based on level 3 inputs. 
(b) Other financial assets and liabilities 
The fair values of cash and cash equivalents, dividends and interest receivable, other assets and receivables, margin 
deposited on swaps and forward contracts, accounts payable and other liabilities, due to the Manager, payable for 
investments purchased and credit facility, distributions payable and redemptions payable approximate their carrying 
amounts due to their short-term maturities. 
(c) Derivatives 
Derivatives contracts are measured at fair value using a market comparison technique, present value of the estimated 
future cash flows or market quotes. Valuations of derivative instruments use normal exchange reporting sources for 
exchange-traded derivatives, similar contracts traded in an active market and the quotes from actual transactions in 
similar instruments. The fair value of interest rate swap, which is non-exchange derivatives, are calculated using 
estimated future cash flows. Estimates of the future cash flows are the sum of contractually fixed future amounts and 
expected variable future amounts. As a result, the fair value of the derivative contracts is based on level 2 inputs. 
(d) Portfolio investments 
As at June 30, 2020, portfolio investments include common equity of publicly-traded companies of $369,897 (December 
31, 2019 - $295,534) (level 1), preferred shares of publicly-traded companies of $35,497 (December 31, 2019 - $37,070) 
(level 1), fixed income securities of $15,144 (December 31, 2019 – $7,391) (level 2) and unquoted private investments 
of $120,301 (December 31, 2019 - $113,791) (level 3).

20. COMMITMENTS AND CONTIGENCIES 
(a) Distributions 
Under the terms of the LPA, cash surpluses are to be distributed to the partners as follows: 
(i) 
First, the General Partner will be entitled to 0.01% of distributions; and 
(ii) 
Second, the balance of the distribution will be allocated amongst the unitholders based upon their 
proportional ownership interest within each given class of units. 
Losses are to be allocated to the limited partners in proportion to their ownership interests. 
(b) Unfunded commitments 
As at June 30, 2020, the Partnership has unadvanced commitments under the mortgage and loan investments 
amounting to $26,865 (December 31, 2019 – $18,048). As at June 30, 2020, the Partnership has unadvanced 
commitments under the private equity investments amounting to $192,062 (December 31, 2019 – $135,988).  
(c) Guarantees 
As at June 30, 2020, the Partnership provided $34,850 (December 31, 2019 - $73,028) in financial guarantees for 
certain investments in Associates and Joint Ventures. The guarantees are in relation to mortgages that the investing 
entities have obtained and are secured by underlying properties. 
(d) Letters of credit 
 As at June 30, 2020, the Partnership provided $3,688 CAD and $1,100 USD (December 31, 2019 – $2,805 CAD 
and nil USD) in letters of credit for certain investments in Joint Ventures. The letters of credit issued are from Credit 
Agreement A.

1. THE 
COMPANY
Timbercreek Four Quadrant 
GP Inc.
(the “
Company
”) was incorporated on September 21, 2011 under the laws of 
the Province of Ontario. The purpose of the Company is 
to act as
a general partner for 
Timbercreek Four Quadrant 
Global Real Estate Partners, Timberc
reek Four Quadrant US LP and Timbercreek Four Quadrant Holdings LP
. 
The address and principal place of business of the Company is 
1133 Yonge Street
, Toronto, Ontario, M4
T 2Y7
.
2.  BASIS OF PRESENTATION (a) Statement of compliance
These 
interim 
unaudited financial statements have been prepared in accordance with International Financial 
Reporting Standards (“IFRS”) as issued by the International Accounting Standards Board (“IASB”). The 
interim
unaudited
financial statements were approved by the Bo
ard of Directors of the 
Company
on October 
20 , 20
20
.
(b) Basis of measurement
These
interim
unaudited financial statements have been prepared on a historical cost basis, except for financial 
assets and financial liabilities at fair value through profit or
loss, which are presented at fair value. 
(c) Functional and presentation currency
The 
Company
’s interim
unaudited financial statements are presented in Canadian dollars, which is the functional 
currency of the 
Company
.
3.
 SUMMARY OF SIGNIFICANT ACCOUNTING 
POLICIES
(a) Financial assets and financial liabilitiesFinancial
assets 
are classified as
measured at
one of the following: (i) 
fair value through profit and loss (“
FVTPL
”)
,
(ii) fair value through other comprehensive income (“
FVOCI
”) or 
( i ii) amortized cost
.  Financial liabilities are 
classified as measured at one of the following: (i) FVTPL or (ii) amortized cost.
The 
Company
records its
portfolio
investment at 
FVTPL
. The 
Company
’s investments are FVTPL
at inception. The 
changes in the investm
ent fair values and related transaction costs are recorded in the 
Company
’s Statement of
Income and
Comprehensive Income.  
Financial assets and financial liabilities 
that are classified as measured
at FVTPL are financial instruments that are 
managed, and 
their performance is evaluated on a fair value basis in accordance with the 
Company
’s documented 
investment strategy. Financial instruments
at FVTPL are neither held to collect contractual cash flows nor held 
both to collect contractual cash flows and to s
ell financial assets. The 
Company
is primarily focused on fair value 
information and uses that information to assess the assets’ performance and to make decisions. All other financial assets and financial liabilities are initially measured at fair value p
lus transaction costs that are 
directly attributable to their acquisition or issuance. Financial assets 
and liabilities 
classified as measured at amortized cost are subsequently measured at amortized 
cost using the effective interest method.
This category includes due from (to) related party. Financial assets 
classified and subsequently measured at FVTPL includes investment in the Company.
(b) Fair value measurementFinancial instruments measured at fair 
value are classified into one of three fair value hierarchy levels, based on 
the lowest level input that is significant to the fair value measurement in its entirety. The inputs or methodologies used for valuing securities are not necessarily an indication
of the risk associated with investing in those 
securities.The three fair value hierarchy levels are as follows:
Level 1
– quoted prices (unadjusted) in active markets for identical assets or liabilities;
Level 2
– inputs other than quoted prices included
in Level 1 that are observable for the asset or liability, 
either directly (i.e., as prices) or indirectly (i.e., derived from prices); and
Level 3
–inputs for the asset or liability that are not based on observable market data (unobservable inputs).
Th
e Company
measures 
portfolio investments
at fair value at each reporting date. Fair value is the price that would 
be received to sell an asset or paid to transfer a liability in an orderly transaction between market participants at the measurement date in 
the principal, or in its absence, the most advantageous market to which the 
Company
has access to at that date.When available, the 
Company measures the fair value of an instrument using the quoted price in an active market 
for that instrument. A market is
regarded as “active” if transactions for the asset or liability take place with sufficient 
frequency and volume to provide pricing information on an ongoing basis. The fair value of financial assets and liabilities traded in active markets (such as public
ly traded derivatives and securities) is based on quoted market 
prices at the close of trading on the reporting date. The 
Company
uses the last traded market price for financial 
assets and financial liabilities where the last traded price falls within that
day’s bid
-ask spread. In circumstances 
where the last traded price is not within the bid
-ask spread, the Manager determines the point within the bid
- ask 
spread that is most representative of fair value based on the specific facts and circumstances. 
For a ssets and liabilities that are recognized in the financial statements on a recurring basis, the 
Company
determines whether transfers have occurred between levels in the hierarchy by re
-assessing categorization (based 
on the lowest level input that is signi
ficant to the fair value measurement as a whole) at the end of each reporting 
period.For the purpose of fair value disclosures, the 
Company
has determined classes of assets and liabilities on the basis 
of the nature, characteristics and risks of the asset
or liability and the level of the fair value hierarchy as explained 
above.
Investment 
fund units are valued at their respective net asset value on each valuation date as these values are the 
most readily and regularly available.
( c ) Portfolio investments
The 
Company’s
portfolio investments include 
its investment in limited partnership units.
Th e fair values of 
portfolio investments are determined with reference to their 
calculated values
at the 
most readily available 
measurement date. Changes in the fair value are recognized in the statements of income and comprehensive income in the year incurr
ed. 
Transaction costs directly attributable to the acquisition of these instruments are recognized in the consolidated statements of income and comprehensive income in the year incurred.
(d) Investment transactions and income recognitionInvestment transa
ctions are accounted for on a trade
-date basis; that is, on the day that a buy or sell order is 
executed. Distributions from investment 
funds are recorded when declared. 
Realized gains and losses from investment transactions are calculated as proceeds of
disposition less their average 
cost. The cost of investments represents the amount paid for each security and is determined on an average
- cost 
basis, excluding transaction costs. 
(e) Income taxesIncome tax expense is comprised of current and 
deferred tax. Income tax expense is recognized in profit or loss 
except to the extent that it relates to items recognized directly in equity or other comprehensive income.Current tax is calculated based on the expected tax payable on taxable income for th
e year using enacted corporate 
income tax rates at the reporting date
and includes any adjustment to tax payable in respect of previous years.  
Deferred tax is calculated based on the expected corporate income tax payable in future periods resulting from 
tax timing differences calculated using future substantively enacted corporate income tax rates applicable in the expected period of reversal. 
( f) Critical accounting judgements and estimatesIn the preparation of these 
interim 
financial statements, the 
ma nagement
has made judgments, estimates and 
assumptions that affect the application of the 
Company
’s accounting policies and the reported amounts of assets, 
liabilities, income and expenses. Actual results may differ from these estimates.In making estimate
s and judgments, the Manager relies on external information and observable conditions where 
possible, supplemented by internal analysis as required. There are no known trends, commitments, events or uncertainties that the 
management
believes will materiall
y affect the methodology or assumptions utilized in 
making those estimates and judgments in these financial statements.

7 .  CAPITAL AND FINANCIAL 
RISK MANAGEMENT
(a) Capital managementThe Company’s capital is
comprised of share capital and retained earnings. 
The Company does not have any externally imposed capital requirements.(b) Financial risk management
The 
Company’s activities expose it to a variety of financial risks that arise as a result of its operat
ing and financing
.
Credit riskCredit risk is the risk that the counterparty to a financial instrument will fail to
discharge an obligation or 
commitment to the 
Company . The maximum exposure to credit risk is represented by the total assets of the 
Company
.The Company has minimal credit risk.
Liquidity riskLiquidity risk is defined as the risk that the 
Company
may not be able to settle or meet its obligation associated 
with financial liabilities. 
The Company has 
minimal
liquidity risk.
Currency riskCurrency risk is the risk that the value of a financial instrument will fluctuate due to changes in foreign exchange rates. Currency risk arises from financial instruments (including cash and cash equivalents) that are denominated in a currency other than 
Canadian dollars, which represents the functional currency of the 
Company
. 
The 
Company
is exposed to currency risk through its investment in 
the foreign p
artnership. At 
June
3 0 , 20
20
, the 
Company has $
1, 60
9 in portfolio investments denominated in foreign 
currency
(December 31, 2019 
- $1,590)
. As 
at June 30
, 20
20 , if the Canadian dollar had strengthened or weakened by 
5 % in relation to all foreign currencies, 
with all variables held constant, the net 
income
of the 
Company
would have decreased or increased, 
respectively, 
by 
$ 80
(December 31
, 201
9
-
$ 80 ). In practice, actual results may differ from this sensitivity analysis and the 
difference could be material. Interest rate riskInterest rate risk arises from the possibility that changes in interest rates will affect future cash flows or fair values 
of financial instruments. The Company does not have any interest
-bearing financial instruments and the Company’s due from related 
party
ha
sno exposure to interest rate risk due to their short
- term nature.
Price riskMarket price risk is the risk that the f
air value or future cash flows of financial instruments will fluctuate because 
of changes in market prices (other than those arising from interest rate risk or currency risk). All investments present a risk of capital loss. The 
Company
is exposed to 
mini
ma
lmarket price risk through its investment in the
portfolio investments
.

GLOSSARY OF TERMS 
“$” means Canadian dollars and “US$” means U.S. dollars. 
“Administrator” means SGGG Fund Services Inc., the administrator for the Partnership, which is responsible for 
providing administration services to the Partnership, including record keeping, register management, Unit transaction 
and similar services as may be provided to the Partnership by the administrator or by such other party as may be 
retained from time to time by the Partnership.

 “Business Day” means any day except Saturday, Sunday, a statutory holiday in Toronto, Ontario or any other day on 
which the Toronto Stock Exchange is not open for trading. 
“CBCA” means the Canada Business Corporations Act. 
“Change in Non Portfolio Assets” for the Partnership on a Valuation Date means: 
(i) 
the aggregate of all income accrued by the Partnership on that Valuation Date, including cash 
dividends and distributions, interest and compensation; plus or minus 
(ii) 
any change in the value of any non-portfolio assets or liabilities stated in any foreign currency 
accrued on that Valuation Date, including, without limitation, cash, accrued dividends or interest 
and any receivable or payables; plus or minus 
(iii) 
any gain or loss resulting from transfers of currencies accrued on that Valuation Date; plus or minus 
(iv) 
any other item accrued on that Valuation Date determined by the General Partner to be relevant in 
determining a Change in Non Portfolio Assets.

“Non-Voting Units” means the Class A NV Units, Class F NV Units, Class I NV Units, Class J NV Units, Class UA 
NV Units. 
“Offering” means the offering of the Class A Units, Class D Units, Class F Units, Class I Units, Class J Units, Class 
UF Units and Class UJ Units pursuant to this Confidential Offering Memorandum. 
“Ordinary Resolution” means a resolution passed by more than 50% of the votes cast at a duly constituted meeting 
of Limited Partners holding Voting Units, or an adjournment thereof, or, alternatively, a written resolution signed in 
one or more counterparts by Limited Partners holding more than 50% of the Voting Units outstanding entitled to vote 
on such resolution at a meeting.

obtained by multiplying that amount by a fraction, the numerator of which is the number of Units of a Class of Units 
of the Partnership registered in the name of that Unitholder and the denominator of which is the total number of Units 
of that Class of the Partnership then outstanding;

“Timbercreek” means the group of companies using the Timbercreek corporate name and their affiliates.  
“Total Assets” means the aggregate value of the assets of the Partnership. 
“Total Return” means the return generated on a Class of Units, including, but not limited to, income from 
distributions declared and taxes (current and deferred) allocated to a Partner, as well as the appreciation or depreciation 
in the Class Net Asset Value per Unit, over the calendar period, calculated on December 31st of each year after any 
deduction of any management fee or trailer fee payable by the Partnership to the Manager pursuant to the Management 
Agreement. In calculating the Total Return in respect of a Unit, if that Unit has not been outstanding for a full year on 
the date of calculation, the amount of Total Return on that Unit shall be pro-rated proportional to the number of days 
for which that Unit has been outstanding. 
“Trailer Fee” has the meaning attributed thereto under “Item 2.7.1 - Limited Partnership Agreement – Fees and 
Expenses – Trailer Fee”. 
“Trust” means Four Quadrant Global Real Estate Trust (formerly, Timbercreek Four Quadrant Global Real Estate 
Trust). 
“Trust NAV” on a particular date will be equal to (i) the aggregate value of the assets of the Trust determined in 
accordance with the valuation principles set out in the Declaration of Trust, less (ii) the consolidated liabilities of the 
Trust (excluding any liabilities already deducted in the determination of the aggregate value of the assets of the Trust). 
“Trust Subscription Agreement” means the standing subscription agreement made among the Partnership, the 
Commercial Trust and the Trust, as may be amended from time to time by the General Partner, on behalf of the 
Partnership, and the other parties thereto, whereby whenever the Partnership receives a Special Redemption Request 
that is determined by the General Partner as eligible, the Trust will directly or indirectly subscribe for the number and 
corresponding Class of Non-Voting Units that is equal in number to the Special Redemption Units, and as 
consideration therefor will issue the appropriate number and class of Trust Units. 
“Trust Unit” means a unit of a class of the Trust. 
“Unitholders” means the holders of the Units. 
“Units” means the Voting Units and the Non-Voting Units.
